# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
FileHandler::writeFile
Summary: Writes the given byte array to a file at the given path, creating any missing parent directories.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that filePath and content are non-null; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  2. Resolve the parent directory of filePath and create any missing directories (recursively).
  3. Write content to filePath, replacing any existing file.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: None
Input: filePath - a non-null String path of the destination file; content - a non-null EByteArray of bytes to write.
Output: void - no return value; side effect is a file written to disk.
Pre-condition: filePath and content are non-null; the process has write permission for the target directory (and parent directories if they must be created).
Post-condition: A file exists at filePath containing exactly the bytes in content; any prior content at that path has been overwritten; missing parent directories now exist.

FileHandler::guessMimeType
Summary: Attempts to infer the MIME type of a file from its path (typically by file extension); falls back to "application/octet-stream" when unknown.
Algorithm:
  1. Convert filePath to a Path and extract the file name.
  2. Look up the lowercased file extension in an internal extension-to-MIME map (e.g., ".txt" -> "text/plain", ".png" -> "image/png", ".json" -> "application/json").
  3. If a mapping is found, return that MIME type.
  4. If no extension or no mapping, return "application/octet-stream".
Related Features: None
Related Classes: None
Input: path - a Path representing a file location on disk.
Output: A String MIME type; one of the known mappings or "application/octet-stream".
Pre-condition: path is non-null (may point to a non-existent file); only the path string is consulted, not file content.
Post-condition: No I/O is performed and no state is mutated.

FileHandler::readFile
Summary: Reads the raw bytes from a file on disk and returns a FileData containing the bytes and an inferred MIME type.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that filePath is non-null; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  2. Open the file at filePath and read all bytes into an EByteArray.
  3. Determine the MIME type by calling guessMimeType(Paths.get(filePath)).
  4. Construct a FileData with the read bytes and inferred MIME type.
  5. Return the FileData.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: FileData
Input: filePath - a non-null String path to an existing readable file.
Output: A FileData whose content holds all bytes of the file and whose mimeType is inferred from the file name (or "application/octet-stream").
Pre-condition: filePath is non-null; the file exists and the process has read permission.
Post-condition: The file is not modified; the returned FileData holds a snapshot of the file content at read time.

FileHandler::createFileData
Summary: Creates a new FileData instance holding the given MIME type and binary content.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that mimeType is non-null; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  2. Validate that content is non-null; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  3. Construct a new FileData with content = content and mimeType = mimeType.
  4. Return the new FileData.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: FileData
Input: mimeType - a non-null String MIME type; content - a non-null EByteArray of file bytes.
Output: A FileData instance encapsulating the given content and MIME type.
Pre-condition: mimeType and content are both non-null.
Post-condition: A FileData object is returned with the provided fields; the input arrays are not defensively copied.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

    processFile(inputPath : String, outputPath : String, operation : String) : EMap
    restoreBinaryData(preparedData : PreparedData) : EByteArray
    restoreBinaryDataFromMap(data : EMap) : EByteArray
    suggestRecipe(size : int) : String
    unprocessData(data : EByteArray) : EByteArray
}

    mimeType : String

    hashCode() : int
    toString() : String
}

    createFileData(mimeType : String, content : EByteArray) : FileData
    guessMimeType(path : Path) : String
    readFile(filePath : String) : FileData
    writeFile(filePath : String, content : EByteArray) : void
}

    content : String
    mimeType : String
    size : int
    toMap() : EMap
}
class VerificationMethod {
    none : String
    sha256 : String
}

@enduml

# Operation Specifications
FileData::equals
Summary: Tests whether this FileData equals another object by comparing both the binary content and the MIME type.
Algorithm:
  1. If o is this, return true.
  2. If o is null or not an instance of FileData, return false.
  3. Compare the other FileData's bytes to this.content using Arrays.equals; if not equal, return false.
  4. Compare mimeType using Objects.equals; return the result.
Related Features: content, mimeType
Related Classes: FileData
Input: o - an EJavaObject (any object) to compare against.
Output: A boolean: true if o is a FileData with identical content bytes and identical mimeType; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: content and mimeType fields are properly initialized (content may be null/empty; mimeType is a String).
Post-condition: No state is mutated; the result is deterministic for the same pair of objects.

FileData::hashCode
Summary: Computes a hash code for this FileData based on both the content bytes and the MIME type, consistent with equals().
Algorithm:
  1. Compute the hash of content using Arrays.hashCode.
  2. Compute the hash of mimeType using Objects.hashCode.
  3. Combine the two values (e.g., result = Objects.hash(contentHash, mimeTypeHash)) and return it.
Related Features: content, mimeType
Related Classes: FileData
Output: An int hash code that is equal for two FileData objects that compare equal via equals().
Pre-condition: content and mimeType fields are properly initialized.
Post-condition: No state is mutated; equal FileData instances produce equal hash codes.

  1. If o is this, return true.
  2. If o is null or not an instance of FileData, return false.
  3. Compare the other FileData's bytes to this.content using Arrays.equals; if not equal, return false.
  4. Compare mimeType using Objects.equals; return the result.
Related Features: content, mimeType
Related Classes: FileData
Input: o - an EJavaObject (any object) to compare against.
Output: A boolean: true if o is a FileData with identical content bytes and identical mimeType; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: content and mimeType fields are properly initialized (content may be null/empty; mimeType is a String).
Post-condition: No state is mutated; the result is deterministic for the same pair of objects.

FileData::hashCode
Summary: Computes a hash code for this FileData based on both the content bytes and the MIME type, consistent with equals().
Algorithm:
  1. Compute the hash of content using Arrays.hashCode.
  2. Compute the hash of mimeType using Objects.hashCode.
  3. Combine the two values (e.g., result = Objects.hash(contentHash, mimeTypeHash)) and return it.
Related Features: content, mimeType
Related Classes: FileData
Output: An int hash code that is equal for two FileData objects that compare equal via equals().
Pre-condition: content and mimeType fields are properly initialized.
Post-condition: No state is mutated; equal FileData instances produce equal hash codes.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;

8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
EmojiCodec::decode
Summary: Decodes an emoji string back into its original UTF-8 text, reversing the encode process including any optional compression.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that emojiData is not null; if null, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
  2. If emojiData is empty, return an empty string.
  3. For each emoji character in emojiData, look up its numeric chunk value in reverseMap; if any emoji is not present in reverseMap, throw an IllegalArgumentException identifying the bad symbol.
  4. Accumulate the bit representations of all chunks (bitsPerChunk bits per symbol) into a contiguous bit stream.
  5. Convert the bit stream into a byte array.
  6. If compression == "zlib", decompress the byte array using zlib; if "none", leave bytes unchanged.
  7. Decode the resulting bytes as a UTF-8 string and return it.
Related Features: baseSize, bitsPerChunk, compression, mask, reverseMap, recipeType, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: emojiData - a String composed only of emoji characters belonging to the active recipe's emoji alphabet (may be empty).
Output: The original UTF-8 text string that was encoded; empty string when emojiData is empty.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; emojiData is non-null; every emoji character in emojiData is present in reverseMap for the active recipe.
Post-condition: A UTF-8 string equivalent to the original input of encode() is returned; the receiver's mappings and configuration are unchanged.

EmojiCodec::encodeBinary
Summary: Encodes a binary payload (byte array plus MIME type) into an emoji string by first serializing it to a JSON-compatible structure (Base64 content, MIME type, size) and then emoji-encoding that serialization.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that mimeType and data are not null; if either is null, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Call prepareBinaryData(mimeType, data) to obtain a PreparedData (content = Base64 string of data, mimeType, size = data.length).
  3. Serialize the PreparedData to a JSON string with fields "content", "mime_type", "size".
  4. Call encode(jsonString) to emoji-encode the JSON string.
  5. Return the resulting emoji string.
Related Features: baseSize, bitsPerChunk, compression, emojiMap, mask, recipeType, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod, PreparedData
Input: mimeType - a non-null String indicating the MIME type of the binary data; data - a non-null EByteArray containing the binary content.
Output: A String of emoji characters representing the binary payload in JSON-Base64 form.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; mimeType and data are non-null.
Post-condition: The returned emoji string can be decoded by decodeBinary() to recover the original byte array and MIME type.

EmojiCodec::getFileInfo
Summary: Inspects a file on disk and returns information describing its size, MIME type, whether it can be processed, and a suggested recipe (or an error entry if it cannot be processed).
Algorithm:
  1. Try to read the file using fileHandler.readFile(filePath) to obtain a FileData containing content bytes and MIME type.
  2. On success, build a result map with keys "size" (byte count), "mime_type", "can_process" = true, and "suggested_recipe" = suggestRecipe(size).
  3. On any failure (missing file, read error), return a result map with "can_process" = false and an "error" message describing the failure.
  4. Return the result map.
Related Features: fileHandler
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData
Input: filePath - a String path to an existing file on disk.
Output: An EMap containing: "size" (int, bytes), "mime_type" (String), "can_process" (boolean), "suggested_recipe" (String: quick/light/classic/gourmet) on success; or "can_process" = false and "error" (String) on failure.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; the caller has appropriate file system permissions; filePath is a syntactically valid path string (the file itself may or may not exist).
Post-condition: No persistent modification is made to the file; the returned map accurately reflects the on-disk state at inspection time.

EmojiCodec::getStats
Summary: Computes encoding statistics comparing an original string with its emoji-encoded representation.
Algorithm:
  1. Compute original_bytes = original.getBytes(UTF-8).length.
  2. Compute encoded_length = the number of emoji characters in encoded (i.e., encoded.length()).
  3. Compute bits_per_emoji = bitsPerChunk for the active recipe (e.g., 6, 7, 8, or 10 for quick/light/classic/gourmet).
  4. Compute theoretical_ratio = (encoded_length * bits_per_emoji) / 8.0  (expected encoded bytes if treated purely as bit-packed output).
  5. Compute actual_ratio = original_bytes / max(encoded_length, 1)  (original bytes per emoji symbol), guarding division by zero.
  6. Return an EMap with keys "original_bytes", "encoded_length", "actual_ratio", "theoretical_ratio", "bits_per_emoji".
Related Features: bitsPerChunk, recipeType
Related Classes: None
Input: original - the original UTF-8 String that was passed to encode(); encoded - the emoji String returned by encode().
Output: An EMap with integer/double entries: original_bytes (int), encoded_length (int), actual_ratio (double), theoretical_ratio (double), bits_per_emoji (int).
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; original and encoded are non-null; encoded was produced by encoding original with the same EmojiCodec.
Post-condition: A populated statistics map is returned; no internal state is mutated.

EmojiCodec::encode
Summary: Encodes a UTF-8 text string into an emoji string, optionally compressing the byte stream first, using the active recipe's emoji alphabet.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that data is not null; if null, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
  2. If data is empty, return an empty string.
  3. Convert data to a UTF-8 byte array.
  4. If compression == "zlib", compress the bytes with zlib; if "none" (or other), leave bytes unchanged.
  5. Convert the (possibly compressed) byte array into a stream of bits.
  6. Split the bit stream into chunks of bitsPerChunk bits each (last chunk zero-padded).
  7. For each chunk, mask it to baseSize - 1 and look up the corresponding emoji in emojiMap.
  8. Concatenate the emojis into the resulting String and return it.
Related Features: baseSize, bitsPerChunk, compression, emojiMap, mask, recipeType, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data - a non-null UTF-8 String to encode (may be empty).
Output: A String composed of emoji characters from the active recipe representing the encoded (and optionally compressed) input.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized (initializeIngredients() called); data is non-null.
Post-condition: The returned emoji string can be decoded by decode() to recover the original UTF-8 text; the receiver configuration is unchanged.

EmojiCodec::calculateHash
Summary: Computes a verification digest for a given byte array according to the currently configured verification method (sha256 or none).
Algorithm:
  1. If verification == "sha256", compute the SHA-256 digest of data, format it as a lowercase hexadecimal string, and zero-pad it to 64 characters.
  2. If verification == "none" (or any other value), return an empty string.
  3. Return the resulting digest.
Related Features: verification, verificationMethod
Related Classes: VerificationMethod
Output: A verification digest string; for sha256 it is a 64-character lowercase hex SHA-256 digest; for none it is an empty string.
Pre-condition: The EmojiCodec instance has been initialized (initializeIngredients() called) so that verification and verificationMethod are set; data is non-null.
Post-condition: A deterministic, correctly formatted digest (or empty string) is returned; the input data is not modified.

EmojiCodec::prepareBinaryData
Summary: Converts a raw byte array and MIME type into a PreparedData object suitable for JSON serialization before emoji encoding.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that mimeType and data are non-null; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  2. Encode data as a Base64 string (standard alphabet, no line breaks).
  3. Create a new PreparedData with content = Base64 string, mimeType = mimeType, size = data.length.
  4. Return the PreparedData.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: mimeType - a non-null String MIME type; data - a non-null EByteArray of binary content.
Output: A PreparedData instance whose fields are content (Base64 String), mimeType (String), size (int).
Pre-condition: mimeType and data are non-null.
Post-condition: The returned PreparedData is ready to be serialized via toMap() and emoji-encoded.

EmojiCodec::suggestRecipe
Summary: Suggests an emoji recipe name appropriate for a file of the given byte size.
Algorithm:
  1. If size < 1024 (1 KB), return "quick".
  2. Else if size < 10240 (10 KB), return "light".
  3. Else if size < 102400 (100 KB), return "classic".
  4. Else return "gourmet".
Related Features: None
Related Classes: None
Input: size - an int byte count of a file (must be >= 0).
Output: A String recipe name: one of "quick", "light", "classic", "gourmet".
Pre-condition: size is a non-negative integer.
Post-condition: A deterministic recipe name is returned; no state is mutated.

EmojiCodec::processData
Summary: Applies the configured compression method to a byte array (zlib compress for "zlib", pass-through for "none").
Algorithm:
  1. If compression == "zlib", deflate-compress data using standard zlib and return the compressed bytes.
  2. Otherwise (compression == "none" or any other value), return data unchanged.
  3. Return the resulting byte array.
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data - a non-null EByteArray to (optionally) compress.
Output: A non-null EByteArray that is either the zlib-compressed form of data or the original data.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; data is non-null.
Post-condition: The returned bytes are either a valid zlib stream or an exact copy of the input.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Summary: Restores raw bytes from a deserialized map representation of binary payload data by Base64-decoding its "content" field.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that data is non-null and contains a "content" key; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  2. Read the "content" string value and Base64-decode it into a byte array.
  3. Return the resulting byte array.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: data - a non-null EMap (deserialized JSON map) containing at minimum a "content" key whose value is a Base64 string.
Output: An EByteArray of the decoded binary content.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; data contains a "content" key with a valid Base64 string value.
Post-condition: The returned bytes equal the original binary content that was encoded; the input map is not mutated.

EmojiCodec::decodeBinary
Summary: Decodes an emoji string representing a binary payload (Base64 content plus MIME type and size) back into a list containing the original byte array and its MIME type.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that encoded is not null; if null, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Call decode(encoded) to obtain the serialized JSON string.
  3. Parse the JSON string into a map containing fields "content" (Base64 string), "mime_type" (string), and "size" (integer).
  4. Decode the "content" Base64 string into a raw byte array.
  5. Return a list [byteArray, mimeType].
Related Features: baseSize, bitsPerChunk, compression, mask, reverseMap, recipeType, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod, PreparedData
Input: encoded - a String of emoji characters representing a previously encoded binary payload.
Output: A list (List<EMap>/List<EJavaObject>) where index 0 is the decoded EByteArray and index 1 is the MIME type String.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; encoded is non-null and was produced by encodeBinary() for the same recipe; the decoded JSON contains valid Base64 under "content" and the expected "mime_type"/"size" fields.
Post-condition: The returned byte array equals the original bytes passed to encodeBinary() and the returned MIME type equals the original MIME type.

EmojiCodec::processFile
Summary: Performs file-oriented encode or decode on a single file according to the requested operation, writing the result to an output path.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that operation is either "encode" or "decode"; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  2. Read the input file via fileHandler.readFile(inputPath) to obtain content bytes and MIME type.
  3. If operation == "encode":
     a. Encode the content bytes by calling encodeBinary(mimeType, content).
     b. Convert the resulting emoji string to UTF-8 bytes.
     c. Build a result map with keys "original_bytes", "encoded_length", "mime_type", "actual_ratio" = original_bytes / max(encoded_length, 1).
     d. Write the UTF-8 bytes of the emoji string to outputPath via fileHandler.writeFile.
  4. If operation == "decode":
     a. Read the emoji string from the input file as UTF-8 bytes and decode it to a String.
     b. Call decodeBinary(emojiString) to obtain [bytes, mimeType].
     c. Build a result map with keys "decoded_size" = bytes.length, "mime_type".
     d. Write the decoded bytes to outputPath via fileHandler.writeFile.
  5. Return the result map.
Related Features: fileHandler
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData, CompressionMethod, PreparedData
Input: inputPath - a String path to an existing input file; outputPath - a String path where the result will be written (parent directories created if missing); operation - "encode" or "decode".
Output: An EMap containing file encoding/decoding statistics (keys vary by operation as described above).
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; operation is "encode" or "decode"; inputPath points to an existing readable file; the process has permission to write to outputPath.
Post-condition: The output file exists at outputPath and contains the encoded emoji bytes (encode) or decoded raw bytes (decode); the input file is unchanged; the returned map reflects the operation's outcome.

EmojiCodec::unprocessData
Summary: Reverses the configured compression on a byte array (zlib decompress for "zlib", pass-through for "none").
Algorithm:
  1. If compression == "zlib", inflate-decompress data using standard zlib and return the decompressed bytes.
  2. Otherwise (compression == "none" or any other value), return data unchanged.
  3. Return the resulting byte array.
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data - a non-null EByteArray that was previously produced by processData() (or is expected to be zlib-compressed).
Output: A non-null EByteArray that is either the zlib-decompressed form of data or the original data unchanged.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; data is non-null; if compression is "zlib", data must be a valid zlib stream.
Post-condition: The returned bytes, when combined with the bit-to-emoji steps in reverse, yield the original input.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryData
Summary: Restores raw bytes from a PreparedData instance by Base64-decoding its content field.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that preparedData is non-null; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  2. Base64-decode preparedData.content into a byte array.
  3. Return the resulting byte array.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: preparedData - a non-null PreparedData whose content field holds a Base64 string.
Output: An EByteArray containing the decoded binary content.
Pre-condition: preparedData is non-null and its content is valid Base64.
Post-condition: The returned bytes equal the original binary input that produced preparedData.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
EmojiCodec::decode
Summary: Decodes an emoji string back into its original UTF-8 text, reversing the encode process including any optional compression.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that emojiData is not null; if null, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
  2. If emojiData is empty, return an empty string.
  3. For each emoji character in emojiData, look up its numeric chunk value in reverseMap; if any emoji is not present in reverseMap, throw an IllegalArgumentException identifying the bad symbol.
  4. Accumulate the bit representations of all chunks (bitsPerChunk bits per symbol) into a contiguous bit stream.
  5. Convert the bit stream into a byte array.
  6. If compression == "zlib", decompress the byte array using zlib; if "none", leave bytes unchanged.
  7. Decode the resulting bytes as a UTF-8 string and return it.
Related Features: baseSize, bitsPerChunk, compression, mask, reverseMap, recipeType, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: emojiData - a String composed only of emoji characters belonging to the active recipe's emoji alphabet (may be empty).
Output: The original UTF-8 text string that was encoded; empty string when emojiData is empty.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; emojiData is non-null; every emoji character in emojiData is present in reverseMap for the active recipe.
Post-condition: A UTF-8 string equivalent to the original input of encode() is returned; the receiver's mappings and configuration are unchanged.

EmojiCodec::encodeBinary
Summary: Encodes a binary payload (byte array plus MIME type) into an emoji string by first serializing it to a JSON-compatible structure (Base64 content, MIME type, size) and then emoji-encoding that serialization.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that mimeType and data are not null; if either is null, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Call prepareBinaryData(mimeType, data) to obtain a PreparedData (content = Base64 string of data, mimeType, size = data.length).
  3. Serialize the PreparedData to a JSON string with fields "content", "mime_type", "size".
  4. Call encode(jsonString) to emoji-encode the JSON string.
  5. Return the resulting emoji string.
Related Features: baseSize, bitsPerChunk, compression, emojiMap, mask, recipeType, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod, PreparedData
Input: mimeType - a non-null String indicating the MIME type of the binary data; data - a non-null EByteArray containing the binary content.
Output: A String of emoji characters representing the binary payload in JSON-Base64 form.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; mimeType and data are non-null.
Post-condition: The returned emoji string can be decoded by decodeBinary() to recover the original byte array and MIME type.

EmojiCodec::getFileInfo
Summary: Inspects a file on disk and returns information describing its size, MIME type, whether it can be processed, and a suggested recipe (or an error entry if it cannot be processed).
Algorithm:
  1. Try to read the file using fileHandler.readFile(filePath) to obtain a FileData containing content bytes and MIME type.
  2. On success, build a result map with keys "size" (byte count), "mime_type", "can_process" = true, and "suggested_recipe" = suggestRecipe(size).
  3. On any failure (missing file, read error), return a result map with "can_process" = false and an "error" message describing the failure.
  4. Return the result map.
Related Features: fileHandler
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData
Input: filePath - a String path to an existing file on disk.
Output: An EMap containing: "size" (int, bytes), "mime_type" (String), "can_process" (boolean), "suggested_recipe" (String: quick/light/classic/gourmet) on success; or "can_process" = false and "error" (String) on failure.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; the caller has appropriate file system permissions; filePath is a syntactically valid path string (the file itself may or may not exist).
Post-condition: No persistent modification is made to the file; the returned map accurately reflects the on-disk state at inspection time.

EmojiCodec::getStats
Summary: Computes encoding statistics comparing an original string with its emoji-encoded representation.
Algorithm:
  1. Compute original_bytes = original.getBytes(UTF-8).length.
  2. Compute encoded_length = the number of emoji characters in encoded (i.e., encoded.length()).
  3. Compute bits_per_emoji = bitsPerChunk for the active recipe (e.g., 6, 7, 8, or 10 for quick/light/classic/gourmet).
  4. Compute theoretical_ratio = (encoded_length * bits_per_emoji) / 8.0  (expected encoded bytes if treated purely as bit-packed output).
  5. Compute actual_ratio = original_bytes / max(encoded_length, 1)  (original bytes per emoji symbol), guarding division by zero.
  6. Return an EMap with keys "original_bytes", "encoded_length", "actual_ratio", "theoretical_ratio", "bits_per_emoji".
Related Features: bitsPerChunk, recipeType
Related Classes: None
Input: original - the original UTF-8 String that was passed to encode(); encoded - the emoji String returned by encode().
Output: An EMap with integer/double entries: original_bytes (int), encoded_length (int), actual_ratio (double), theoretical_ratio (double), bits_per_emoji (int).
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; original and encoded are non-null; encoded was produced by encoding original with the same EmojiCodec.
Post-condition: A populated statistics map is returned; no internal state is mutated.

EmojiCodec::encode
Summary: Encodes a UTF-8 text string into an emoji string, optionally compressing the byte stream first, using the active recipe's emoji alphabet.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that data is not null; if null, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
  2. If data is empty, return an empty string.
  3. Convert data to a UTF-8 byte array.
  4. If compression == "zlib", compress the bytes with zlib; if "none" (or other), leave bytes unchanged.
  5. Convert the (possibly compressed) byte array into a stream of bits.
  6. Split the bit stream into chunks of bitsPerChunk bits each (last chunk zero-padded).
  7. For each chunk, mask it to baseSize - 1 and look up the corresponding emoji in emojiMap.
  8. Concatenate the emojis into the resulting String and return it.
Related Features: baseSize, bitsPerChunk, compression, emojiMap, mask, recipeType, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data - a non-null UTF-8 String to encode (may be empty).
Output: A String composed of emoji characters from the active recipe representing the encoded (and optionally compressed) input.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized (initializeIngredients() called); data is non-null.
Post-condition: The returned emoji string can be decoded by decode() to recover the original UTF-8 text; the receiver configuration is unchanged.

EmojiCodec::calculateHash
Summary: Computes a verification digest for a given byte array according to the currently configured verification method (sha256 or none).
Algorithm:
  1. If verification == "sha256", compute the SHA-256 digest of data, format it as a lowercase hexadecimal string, and zero-pad it to 64 characters.
  2. If verification == "none" (or any other value), return an empty string.
  3. Return the resulting digest.
Related Features: verification, verificationMethod
Related Classes: VerificationMethod
Output: A verification digest string; for sha256 it is a 64-character lowercase hex SHA-256 digest; for none it is an empty string.
Pre-condition: The EmojiCodec instance has been initialized (initializeIngredients() called) so that verification and verificationMethod are set; data is non-null.
Post-condition: A deterministic, correctly formatted digest (or empty string) is returned; the input data is not modified.

EmojiCodec::prepareBinaryData
Summary: Converts a raw byte array and MIME type into a PreparedData object suitable for JSON serialization before emoji encoding.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that mimeType and data are non-null; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  2. Encode data as a Base64 string (standard alphabet, no line breaks).
  3. Create a new PreparedData with content = Base64 string, mimeType = mimeType, size = data.length.
  4. Return the PreparedData.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: mimeType - a non-null String MIME type; data - a non-null EByteArray of binary content.
Output: A PreparedData instance whose fields are content (Base64 String), mimeType (String), size (int).
Pre-condition: mimeType and data are non-null.
Post-condition: The returned PreparedData is ready to be serialized via toMap() and emoji-encoded.

EmojiCodec::suggestRecipe
Summary: Suggests an emoji recipe name appropriate for a file of the given byte size.
Algorithm:
  1. If size < 1024 (1 KB), return "quick".
  2. Else if size < 10240 (10 KB), return "light".
  3. Else if size < 102400 (100 KB), return "classic".
  4. Else return "gourmet".
Related Features: None
Related Classes: None
Input: size - an int byte count of a file (must be >= 0).
Output: A String recipe name: one of "quick", "light", "classic", "gourmet".
Pre-condition: size is a non-negative integer.
Post-condition: A deterministic recipe name is returned; no state is mutated.

EmojiCodec::processData
Summary: Applies the configured compression method to a byte array (zlib compress for "zlib", pass-through for "none").
Algorithm:
  1. If compression == "zlib", deflate-compress data using standard zlib and return the compressed bytes.
  2. Otherwise (compression == "none" or any other value), return data unchanged.
  3. Return the resulting byte array.
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data - a non-null EByteArray to (optionally) compress.
Output: A non-null EByteArray that is either the zlib-compressed form of data or the original data.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; data is non-null.
Post-condition: The returned bytes are either a valid zlib stream or an exact copy of the input.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Summary: Restores raw bytes from a deserialized map representation of binary payload data by Base64-decoding its "content" field.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that data is non-null and contains a "content" key; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  2. Read the "content" string value and Base64-decode it into a byte array.
  3. Return the resulting byte array.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: data - a non-null EMap (deserialized JSON map) containing at minimum a "content" key whose value is a Base64 string.
Output: An EByteArray of the decoded binary content.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; data contains a "content" key with a valid Base64 string value.
Post-condition: The returned bytes equal the original binary content that was encoded; the input map is not mutated.

EmojiCodec::decodeBinary
Summary: Decodes an emoji string representing a binary payload (Base64 content plus MIME type and size) back into a list containing the original byte array and its MIME type.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that encoded is not null; if null, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Call decode(encoded) to obtain the serialized JSON string.
  3. Parse the JSON string into a map containing fields "content" (Base64 string), "mime_type" (string), and "size" (integer).
  4. Decode the "content" Base64 string into a raw byte array.
  5. Return a list [byteArray, mimeType].
Related Features: baseSize, bitsPerChunk, compression, mask, reverseMap, recipeType, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod, PreparedData
Input: encoded - a String of emoji characters representing a previously encoded binary payload.
Output: A list (List<EMap>/List<EJavaObject>) where index 0 is the decoded EByteArray and index 1 is the MIME type String.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; encoded is non-null and was produced by encodeBinary() for the same recipe; the decoded JSON contains valid Base64 under "content" and the expected "mime_type"/"size" fields.
Post-condition: The returned byte array equals the original bytes passed to encodeBinary() and the returned MIME type equals the original MIME type.

EmojiCodec::processFile
Summary: Performs file-oriented encode or decode on a single file according to the requested operation, writing the result to an output path.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that operation is either "encode" or "decode"; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  2. Read the input file via fileHandler.readFile(inputPath) to obtain content bytes and MIME type.
  3. If operation == "encode":
     a. Encode the content bytes by calling encodeBinary(mimeType, content).
     b. Convert the resulting emoji string to UTF-8 bytes.
     c. Build a result map with keys "original_bytes", "encoded_length", "mime_type", "actual_ratio" = original_bytes / max(encoded_length, 1).
     d. Write the UTF-8 bytes of the emoji string to outputPath via fileHandler.writeFile.
  4. If operation == "decode":
     a. Read the emoji string from the input file as UTF-8 bytes and decode it to a String.
     b. Call decodeBinary(emojiString) to obtain [bytes, mimeType].
     c. Build a result map with keys "decoded_size" = bytes.length, "mime_type".
     d. Write the decoded bytes to outputPath via fileHandler.writeFile.
  5. Return the result map.
Related Features: fileHandler
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData, CompressionMethod, PreparedData
Input: inputPath - a String path to an existing input file; outputPath - a String path where the result will be written (parent directories created if missing); operation - "encode" or "decode".
Output: An EMap containing file encoding/decoding statistics (keys vary by operation as described above).
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; operation is "encode" or "decode"; inputPath points to an existing readable file; the process has permission to write to outputPath.
Post-condition: The output file exists at outputPath and contains the encoded emoji bytes (encode) or decoded raw bytes (decode); the input file is unchanged; the returned map reflects the operation's outcome.

EmojiCodec::unprocessData
Summary: Reverses the configured compression on a byte array (zlib decompress for "zlib", pass-through for "none").
Algorithm:
  1. If compression == "zlib", inflate-decompress data using standard zlib and return the decompressed bytes.
  2. Otherwise (compression == "none" or any other value), return data unchanged.
  3. Return the resulting byte array.
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data - a non-null EByteArray that was previously produced by processData() (or is expected to be zlib-compressed).
Output: A non-null EByteArray that is either the zlib-decompressed form of data or the original data unchanged.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; data is non-null; if compression is "zlib", data must be a valid zlib stream.
Post-condition: The returned bytes, when combined with the bit-to-emoji steps in reverse, yield the original input.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryData
Summary: Restores raw bytes from a PreparedData instance by Base64-decoding its content field.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that preparedData is non-null; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  2. Base64-decode preparedData.content into a byte array.
  3. Return the resulting byte array.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: preparedData - a non-null PreparedData whose content field holds a Base64 string.
Output: An EByteArray containing the decoded binary content.
Pre-condition: preparedData is non-null and its content is valid Base64.
Post-condition: The returned bytes equal the original binary input that produced preparedData.

 * revised description for operation_1
 */

 * revised description for this operation_2
 */

...

  2. Read the input file via fileHandler.readFile(inputPath) to obtain content bytes and MIME type.
  3. If operation == "encode":
     a. Encode the content bytes by calling encodeBinary(mimeType, content).
     b. Convert the resulting emoji string to UTF-8 bytes.
     c. Build a result map with keys "original_bytes", "encoded_length", "mime_type", "actual_ratio" = original_bytes / max(encoded_length, 1).
     d. Write the UTF-8 bytes of the emoji string to outputPath via fileHandler.writeFile.
  4. If operation == "decode":
     a. Read the emoji string from the input file as UTF-8 bytes and decode it to a String.
     b. Call decodeBinary(emojiString) to obtain [bytes, mimeType].
     c. Build a result map with keys "decoded_size" = bytes.length, "mime_type".
     d. Write the decoded bytes to outputPath via fileHandler.writeFile.
  5. Return the result map.
Related Features: fileHandler
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData
Input: inputPath - a String path to an existing input file; outputPath - a String path where the result will be written (parent directories created if missing); operation - "encode" or "decode".
Output: An EMap containing file encoding/decoding statistics (keys vary by operation as described above).
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; operation is "encode" or "decode"; inputPath points to an existing readable file; the process has permission to write to outputPath.
Post-condition: The output file exists at outputPath and contains the encoded emoji bytes (encode) or decoded raw bytes (decode); the input file is unchanged; the returned map reflects the operation's outcome.}

You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;

8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: FreddyRodgers_emojichef

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system encodes text and binary files into emoji strings and decodes them back to their original data. It treats emoji characters as symbols in a configurable base alphabet, allowing ordinary bytes to be represented as compact emoji sequences. The system also supports optional compression, optional integrity hashing, file MIME preservation, and simple file-oriented encode/decode workflows.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Recipe selection controls the emoji alphabet size. The supported recipe names are `quick`, `light`, `classic`, and `gourmet`, with increasing capacities of 64, 128, 256, and 1024 symbols. The classic recipe represents 8 bits per emoji. Unknown recipes should fall back to classic behavior.

Text encoding should use UTF-8 bytes, optionally compress the byte stream, split the resulting bits into recipe-sized chunks, and map each chunk to an emoji symbol. Decoding should reverse this process and reject null input or symbols that are not part of the active emoji alphabet. Empty text should round-trip as an empty emoji string.

Compression mode `zlib` should use standard zlib-compatible compression and decompression. Compression mode `none` should leave bytes unchanged. Verification mode `sha256` should produce a lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 digest with zero padding; verification mode `none` should produce an empty digest.

Binary data should be wrapped in a JSON-compatible structure before emoji encoding. The binary content is stored as Base64 text, and MIME type and original byte size must be preserved. The serialized field names are `content`, `mime_type`, and `size`. Decoding binary emoji data should restore the byte array and MIME type.

Encoding statistics should report `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `actual_ratio`, `theoretical_ratio`, and `bits_per_emoji`. File encoding results should include `original_bytes`, `encoded_length`, `mime_type`, and `actual_ratio`; file decoding results should include `decoded_size` and `mime_type`. File information results should include size, MIME type, whether the file can be processed, and a suggested recipe, or an error with `can_process` set to false.

Recipe suggestion is based on file size: small files below 1 KB use `quick`, files below 10 KB use `light`, files below 100 KB use `classic`, and larger files use `gourmet`.

File handling should read raw bytes, infer MIME type from the file name when possible, fall back to `application/octet-stream` when unknown, and write bytes while creating missing parent directories.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
EmojiCodec::decode
Summary: Decodes an emoji string back into its original UTF-8 text, reversing the encode process including any optional compression.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that emojiData is not null; if null, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
  2. If emojiData is empty, return an empty string.
  3. For each emoji character in emojiData, look up its numeric chunk value in reverseMap; if any emoji is not present in reverseMap, throw an IllegalArgumentException identifying the bad symbol.
  4. Accumulate the bit representations of all chunks (bitsPerChunk bits per symbol) into a contiguous bit stream.
  5. Convert the bit stream into a byte array.
  6. If compression == "zlib", decompress the byte array using zlib; if "none", leave bytes unchanged.
  7. Decode the resulting bytes as a UTF-8 string and return it.
Related Features: baseSize, bitsPerChunk, compression, mask, reverseMap, recipeType, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: emojiData - a String composed only of emoji characters belonging to the active recipe's emoji alphabet (may be empty).
Output: The original UTF-8 text string that was encoded; empty string when emojiData is empty.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; emojiData is non-null; every emoji character in emojiData is present in reverseMap for the active recipe.
Post-condition: A UTF-8 string equivalent to the original input of encode() is returned; the receiver's mappings and configuration are unchanged.

EmojiCodec::encodeBinary
Summary: Encodes a binary payload (byte array plus MIME type) into an emoji string by first serializing it to a JSON-compatible structure (Base64 content, MIME type, size) and then emoji-encoding that serialization.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that mimeType and data are not null; if either is null, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Call prepareBinaryData(mimeType, data) to obtain a PreparedData (content = Base64 string of data, mimeType, size = data.length).
  3. Serialize the PreparedData to a JSON string with fields "content", "mime_type", "size".
  4. Call encode(jsonString) to emoji-encode the JSON string.
  5. Return the resulting emoji string.
Related Features: baseSize, bitsPerChunk, compression, emojiMap, mask, recipeType, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod, PreparedData
Input: mimeType - a non-null String indicating the MIME type of the binary data; data - a non-null EByteArray containing the binary content.
Output: A String of emoji characters representing the binary payload in JSON-Base64 form.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; mimeType and data are non-null.
Post-condition: The returned emoji string can be decoded by decodeBinary() to recover the original byte array and MIME type.

EmojiCodec::getFileInfo
Summary: Inspects a file on disk and returns information describing its size, MIME type, whether it can be processed, and a suggested recipe (or an error entry if it cannot be processed).
Algorithm:
  1. Try to read the file using fileHandler.readFile(filePath) to obtain a FileData containing content bytes and MIME type.
  2. On success, build a result map with keys "size" (byte count), "mime_type", "can_process" = true, and "suggested_recipe" = suggestRecipe(size).
  3. On any failure (missing file, read error), return a result map with "can_process" = false and an "error" message describing the failure.
  4. Return the result map.
Related Features: fileHandler
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData
Input: filePath - a String path to an existing file on disk.
Output: An EMap containing: "size" (int, bytes), "mime_type" (String), "can_process" (boolean), "suggested_recipe" (String: quick/light/classic/gourmet) on success; or "can_process" = false and "error" (String) on failure.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; the caller has appropriate file system permissions; filePath is a syntactically valid path string (the file itself may or may not exist).
Post-condition: No persistent modification is made to the file; the returned map accurately reflects the on-disk state at inspection time.

EmojiCodec::getStats
Summary: Computes encoding statistics comparing an original string with its emoji-encoded representation.
Algorithm:
  1. Compute original_bytes = original.getBytes(UTF-8).length.
  2. Compute encoded_length = the number of emoji characters in encoded (i.e., encoded.length()).
  3. Compute bits_per_emoji = bitsPerChunk for the active recipe (e.g., 6, 7, 8, or 10 for quick/light/classic/gourmet).
  4. Compute theoretical_ratio = (encoded_length * bits_per_emoji) / 8.0  (expected encoded bytes if treated purely as bit-packed output).
  5. Compute actual_ratio = original_bytes / max(encoded_length, 1)  (original bytes per emoji symbol), guarding division by zero.
  6. Return an EMap with keys "original_bytes", "encoded_length", "actual_ratio", "theoretical_ratio", "bits_per_emoji".
Related Features: bitsPerChunk, recipeType
Related Classes: None
Input: original - the original UTF-8 String that was passed to encode(); encoded - the emoji String returned by encode().
Output: An EMap with integer/double entries: original_bytes (int), encoded_length (int), actual_ratio (double), theoretical_ratio (double), bits_per_emoji (int).
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; original and encoded are non-null; encoded was produced by encoding original with the same EmojiCodec.
Post-condition: A populated statistics map is returned; no internal state is mutated.

EmojiCodec::encode
Summary: Encodes a UTF-8 text string into an emoji string, optionally compressing the byte stream first, using the active recipe's emoji alphabet.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that data is not null; if null, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
  2. If data is empty, return an empty string.
  3. Convert data to a UTF-8 byte array.
  4. If compression == "zlib", compress the bytes with zlib; if "none" (or other), leave bytes unchanged.
  5. Convert the (possibly compressed) byte array into a stream of bits.
  6. Split the bit stream into chunks of bitsPerChunk bits each (last chunk zero-padded).
  7. For each chunk, mask it to baseSize - 1 and look up the corresponding emoji in emojiMap.
  8. Concatenate the emojis into the resulting String and return it.
Related Features: baseSize, bitsPerChunk, compression, emojiMap, mask, recipeType, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data - a non-null UTF-8 String to encode (may be empty).
Output: A String composed of emoji characters from the active recipe representing the encoded (and optionally compressed) input.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized (initializeIngredients() called); data is non-null.
Post-condition: The returned emoji string can be decoded by decode() to recover the original UTF-8 text; the receiver configuration is unchanged.

EmojiCodec::calculateHash
Summary: Computes a verification digest for a given byte array according to the currently configured verification method (sha256 or none).
Algorithm:
  1. If verification == "sha256", compute the SHA-256 digest of data, format it as a lowercase hexadecimal string, and zero-pad it to 64 characters.
  2. If verification == "none" (or any other value), return an empty string.
  3. Return the resulting digest.
Related Features: verification, verificationMethod
Related Classes: VerificationMethod
Input: data - a non-null EByteArray whose digest should be computed.
Output: A verification digest string; for sha256 it is a 64-character lowercase hex SHA-256 digest; for none it is an empty string.
Pre-condition: The EmojiCodec instance has been initialized (initializeIngredients() called) so that verification and verificationMethod are set; data is non-null.
Post-condition: A deterministic, correctly formatted digest (or empty string) is returned; the input data is not modified.

EmojiCodec::prepareBinaryData
Summary: Converts a raw byte array and MIME type into a PreparedData object suitable for JSON serialization before emoji encoding.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that mimeType and data are non-null; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  2. Encode data as a Base64 string (standard alphabet, no line breaks).
  3. Create a new PreparedData with content = Base64 string, mimeType = mimeType, size = data.length.
  4. Return the PreparedData.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: mimeType - a non-null String MIME type; data - a non-null EByteArray of binary content.
Output: A PreparedData instance whose fields are content (Base64 String), mimeType (String), size (int).
Pre-condition: mimeType and data are non-null.
Post-condition: The returned PreparedData is ready to be serialized via toMap() and emoji-encoded.

EmojiCodec::suggestRecipe
Summary: Suggests an emoji recipe name appropriate for a file of the given byte size.
Algorithm:
  1. If size < 1024 (1 KB), return "quick".
  2. Else if size < 10240 (10 KB), return "light".
  3. Else if size < 102400 (100 KB), return "classic".
  4. Else return "gourmet".
Related Features: None
Related Classes: None
Input: size - an int byte count of a file (must be >= 0).
Output: A String recipe name: one of "quick", "light", "classic", "gourmet".
Pre-condition: size is a non-negative integer.
Post-condition: A deterministic recipe name is returned; no state is mutated.

EmojiCodec::processData
Summary: Applies the configured compression method to a byte array (zlib compress for "zlib", pass-through for "none").
Algorithm:
  1. If compression == "zlib", deflate-compress data using standard zlib and return the compressed bytes.
  2. Otherwise (compression == "none" or any other value), return data unchanged.
  3. Return the resulting byte array.
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data - a non-null EByteArray to (optionally) compress.
Output: A non-null EByteArray that is either the zlib-compressed form of data or the original data.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; data is non-null.
Post-condition: The returned bytes are either a valid zlib stream or an exact copy of the input.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryDataFromMap
Summary: Restores raw bytes from a deserialized map representation of binary payload data by Base64-decoding its "content" field.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that data is non-null and contains a "content" key; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  2. Read the "content" string value and Base64-decode it into a byte array.
  3. Return the resulting byte array.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: data - a non-null EMap (deserialized JSON map) containing at minimum a "content" key whose value is a Base64 string.
Output: An EByteArray of the decoded binary content.
Pre-condition: data is non-null; data contains a "content" key with a valid Base64 string value.
Post-condition: The returned bytes equal the original binary content that was encoded; the input map is not mutated.

EmojiCodec::decodeBinary
Summary: Decodes an emoji string representing a binary payload (Base64 content plus MIME type and size) back into a list containing the original byte array and its MIME type.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that encoded is not null; if null, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Call decode(encoded) to obtain the serialized JSON string.
  3. Parse the JSON string into a map containing fields "content" (Base64 string), "mime_type" (string), and "size" (integer).
  4. Decode the "content" Base64 string into a raw byte array.
  5. Return a list [byteArray, mimeType].
Related Features: baseSize, bitsPerChunk, compression, mask, reverseMap, recipeType, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod, PreparedData
Input: encoded - a String of emoji characters representing a previously encoded binary payload.
Output: A list (List<EJavaObject>) where index 0 is the decoded EByteArray and index 1 is the MIME type String.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; encoded is non-null and was produced by encodeBinary() for the same recipe; the decoded JSON contains valid Base64 under "content" and the expected "mime_type"/"size" fields.
Post-condition: The returned byte array equals the original bytes passed to encodeBinary() and the returned MIME type equals the original MIME type.

EmojiCodec::processFile
Summary: Performs file-oriented encode or decode on a single file according to the requested operation, writing the result to an output path.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that operation is either "encode" or "decode"; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  2. Read the input file via fileHandler.readFile(inputPath) to obtain content bytes and MIME type.
  3. If operation == "encode":
     a. Encode the content bytes by calling encodeBinary(mimeType, content).
     b. Convert the resulting emoji string to UTF-8 bytes.
     c. Build a result map with keys "original_bytes", "encoded_length", "mime_type", "actual_ratio" = original_bytes / max(encoded_length, 1).
     d. Write the UTF-8 bytes of the emoji string to outputPath via fileHandler.writeFile.
  4. If operation == "decode":
     a. Read the emoji string from the input file as UTF-8 bytes and decode it to a String.
     b. Call decodeBinary(emojiString) to obtain [bytes, mimeType].
     c. Build a result map with keys "decoded_size" = bytes.length, "mime_type".
     d. Write the decoded bytes to outputPath via fileHandler.writeFile.
  5. Return the result map.
Related Features: fileHandler
Related Classes: FileHandler, FileData
Input: inputPath - a String path to an existing input file; outputPath - a String path where the result will be written (parent directories created if missing); operation - "encode" or "decode".
Output: An EMap containing file encoding/decoding statistics (keys vary by operation as described above).
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; operation is "encode" or "decode"; inputPath points to an existing readable file; the process has permission to write to outputPath.
Post-condition: The output file exists at outputPath and contains the encoded emoji bytes (encode) or decoded raw bytes (decode); the input file is unchanged; the returned map reflects the operation's outcome.

EmojiCodec::unprocessData
Summary: Reverses the configured compression on a byte array (zlib decompress for "zlib", pass-through for "none").
Algorithm:
  1. If compression == "zlib", inflate-decompress data using standard zlib and return the decompressed bytes.
  2. Otherwise (compression == "none" or any other value), return data unchanged.
  3. Return the resulting byte array.
Related Features: compression, compressionMethod
Related Classes: CompressionMethod
Input: data - a non-null EByteArray that was previously produced by processData() (or is expected to be zlib-compressed).
Output: A non-null EByteArray that is either the zlib-decompressed form of data or the original data unchanged.
Pre-condition: EmojiCodec has been initialized; data is non-null; if compression is "zlib", data must be a valid zlib stream.
Post-condition: The returned bytes, when combined with the bit-to-emoji steps in reverse, yield the original input.

EmojiCodec::restoreBinaryData
Summary: Restores raw bytes from a PreparedData instance by Base64-decoding its content field.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate that preparedData is non-null; throw IllegalArgumentException otherwise.
  2. Base64-decode preparedData.content into a byte array.
  3. Return the resulting byte array.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: PreparedData
Input: preparedData - a non-null PreparedData whose content field holds a Base64 string.
Output: An EByteArray containing the decoded binary content.
Pre-condition: preparedData is non-null and its content is valid Base64.
Post-condition: The returned bytes equal the original binary input that produced preparedData.
